My mind has been so focused on my weight and work that I just haven't kept track of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience an intense fear of gaining weight or becoming fat, even though others have told you that your weight is already too low?\n\nPatient: Yes, I get absolutely panicky if the scale goes up even half a pound because I worry everyone at the warehouse will see me as lazy and unprofessional. Even though the doctor says my weight is too low, I honestly think they are overreacting, and hearing that I look tiny actually makes me feel like I'm finally doing something right.\n\nDoctor: Could you share your current height and weight with me, as well as how much weight you have lost recently?\n\nPatient: I don't really know my exact height or weight off the top of my head, and I'm not sure exactly how many pounds I've lost. I just know that my clothes are hanging off me now and people at work and church keep saying I look very small.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven't been keeping close track, do you remember roughly when you had your last menstrual period, or if you've had one at all in the last three months?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't remember, and I'm really not sure if I've had one in the last three months. I've just been so focused on my routine and trying to keep my energy up for work that I haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Besides restricting your food intake, do you use other methods to control your weight, such as exercising excessively, making yourself throw up, or using laxatives or diet pills?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of those things. I've never used laxatives, made myself throw up, taken diet pills, or exercised excessively. I only control my weight by strictly restricting how much food I eat.\n\nDoctor: Since you mentioned tracking the scale closely and worrying about even a half-pound increase, what was the weight on the scale the last time you stepped on it?\n\nPatient: I don't remember the exact number on the scale from the last time I stepped on it. I try not to focus too much on the specific number itself, I just get really anxious if I see it go up at all."
  }

I just know that my clothes are hanging off me now and people at work and church keep saying I look very small.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven't been keeping close track, do you remember roughly when you had your last menstrual period, or if you've had one at all in the last three months?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't remember, and I'm really not sure if I've had one in the last three months. I've just been so focused on my routine and trying to keep my energy up for work that I haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Besides restricting your food intake, do you use other methods to control your weight, such as exercising excessively, making yourself throw up, or using laxatives or diet pills?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of those things. I've never used laxatives, made myself throw up, taken diet pills, or exercised excessively. I only control my weight by strictly restricting how much food I eat.\n\nDoctor: Since you mentioned tracking the scale closely and worrying about even a half-pound increase, what was the weight on the scale the last time you stepped on it?\n\nPatient: I don't remember the exact number on the scale from the last time I stepped on it. I try not to focus too much on the specific number itself, I just get really anxious if I see it go up at all.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror, do you perceive yourself as overweight or feel that certain parts of your body are too big, even though others tell you that you are very thin?\n\nPatient: Yes, when I look in the mirror at home or in the locker room, all I can see are my stomach and thighs looking too big. Even though others say I look tiny, seeing those parts makes me feel like I'm not disciplined enough, which just reminds me of why I was passed over for that promotion. I feel like I have to stay as small as possible to be worth anything.\n\nDoctor: When your doctor told you that your weight was too low, did they happen to mention your specific weight, BMI, or how much underweight you are?\n\nPatient: I don't remember the exact weight or BMI numbers they mentioned. I didn't really pay much attention to it because I felt like they were just overreacting anyway.\n\nDoctor: What was your typical weight before you started strictly restricting your food, or what clothing size did you wear back then compared to now?\n\nPatient: I don't really remember my exact weight or what specific clothing sizes I used to wear before. I just know that my current clothes are noticeably hanging off me now and feel way too big.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed other physical changes, like feeling constantly cold even when it's warm, or having dizzy spells when you stand up?"

Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.
